ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e5859	Madam President, Ladies and gentlemen! If one connects the two terms “social state” and “immigration” with one another, then one can only do so with “instead”: Social state instead of immigration. Why? – We have been building our social state over the last decades, but we are experiencing today that this social state is seriously at risk. We experience one of the left-wing lies in our Republic. It has been said for decades again and again: Further immigration is needed so that the social state can be maintained in order to secure pensions. [[Abg. Oils: Who says that?]] - You've been saying that for years! For years now, you have been claiming that more immigration will ensure all of this – and we are experiencing the opposite today. That's why you also have to clean up these left-wing lies and also have to address these left-wing lies. The social state is not secured by immigration. If that were the case, today our social pots would have to be full, filled to the ground because of the mass immigration we have had to experience over the last 20 years. But the opposite is the case! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] When you look at the facts, it is precisely the picture that the previous mass immigration, as lived by the respective responsible government, takes out more of the social pots than is paid by immigrants. In the annual average 2006, the share of foreign emergency aid beneficiaries in Austria was 14.1 percent. In 2006, €127.2 million of social security contributions were spent on these emergency relief recipients without citizenship. In 2006, the share of foreign people with a maximum compulsory school degree in all job seekers was on average 29.2 percent. A total of € 194.5 million was spent on job-seekers without citizenship with a maximum compulsory school degree in 2006 on unemployment benefit and emergency aid, including social security contributions. The proportion of registered unemployed without citizenship in total registered unemployed in 2006 was an average of 17.6 percent, which is significantly above the proportion of foreigners present in Austria today. [[Abg. Grossruck: What is it about?]] The AMS has, of course, also provided interesting support programmes. There are only 47 000 people with non-Austrian citizenship who have been granted AMS funding – an increase of 22 percent compared to the previous year. We always say that we finally want to have cost truth; Cost truth is also required here in the House. We wanted to know – but this request that we have made was rejected by all the other political groups; unfortunately, God – and have insisted on finally ensuring that we know what immigrants are pouring into our social pots and what they are taking out, but then you refuse to do so! There is a very conscious refusal, because one has no interest in this cost-truth, because it seems that one wants to continue to hide against Austrian citizens what reality looks like. That's exactly the point: We say we want to secure the welfare state for Austria. This is only possible if we make changes here, if we stop immigration on the one hand and finally establish a host worker social insurance in Austria on the other. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] If we try to do so and ensure that there is a fundamental change in our social system, so that our social system is directed primarily in such a way as to benefit Austrian citizens and not every immigrant from the first day. This is a necessary change that we call for. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Oils.]] I know, Mr Oellinger, that you shout and wake up, because of course this does not correspond to your intentions. But we see this need for Austria to maintain the social state permanently and ensure it for Austrians. There isn't!]] But the European Union is moving in this direction with its guidelines, and this is being followed by red and black with a progressive obedience, as we have seen in Austria and, above all, in Vienna. Oils: Here you go!]] [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] This is exactly the method that is being used in the social state of Austria today! But for 20 years – one has to stress and hold this over and over again – the politicians responsible for red and black have known for 20 years that we have low birth rates in Austria. Worm: Childcare facilities,...!]] – Because you can afford fewer and fewer children from the Austrian families. If you have two children today, you can easily fall below the poverty line in Austria; and that is the point. Not to mention a third and fourth child, because then you are safe under the poverty line. That's exactly where you should have started 20 years ago. We had to use our Austrian taxpayers 20 years ago to ensure that Austrian families were finally strengthened and supported so that they could afford a third and fourth child again. [[Abg. Strache: The study published in the newspaper “Presse” for example!]] I should like to suggest to the ladies and gentlemen of FPÖ, for example, the study on the integration of foreigners in Austria, by Felder, Hofer, Schuh und Strohner, from October 2004, and I can quote from it: “This means” – the authors conclude – “that immigration makes a net contribution to the social system due to the more favourable age structure of immigrants. This is referred to as the direct contribution of immigration.’ In a similar study from 2004, by Professor Dr. Gudrun Biffl of the Austrian Institute for Economic Research, I quote: “This was the background for examining the effect of migration on the Austrian welfare system in the 1990s... The research results suggest that the payments of migrants into the social security system and the disbursements to them in total in the 1990s were more or less balanced.” [[Abg. Strache: The daily “Press” has published a completely different study!]] Furthermore, as a reason for this – contrary to what you, Mr. Club Chairman, have formulated about the different unemployment burden – the average duration of an unemployment period among foreigners – Mr. Klubobmann Strache, that is perhaps interesting for you – is more interesting than among locals, since only persons with a license of exemption or proof of establishment are equal to nationals. This fact explains why, on average, the disbursements in the area of unemployment benefit between foreigners and nationals per capita are only slightly different. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Graf.]] [[Further calls at the FPÖ.]] Furthermore, the conclusion here is that further investigations – which you also call for – must always take into account a special context, because – I quote –: “An answer to the question of the scale of the net effect of immigration on social budgets, which is valid at any time and in every constellation, can only be given under certain assumptions. This is explained by the fact that the effect depends on the structure of immigration (age, gender, qualification, migration policy status) at the time of immigration, as well as on the development of income and family situation during the period of residence and life expectancy." The reality is often a little more shady, a little more diverse than one can understand with a too one-dimensional way of thinking. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Do you also have your own opinion?]] The conclusion that Professor Biffl draws here is – and with that I end the quotes – that: “When we look at the structure of immigration to Austria, migrants are currently paying about as much in the social budgets as they take out. This should not be particularly surprising in view of the comparatively low redistribution effect of the Austrian social security system.’ [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Perhaps the professor should become a Minister of Social Affairs!]] [[Further calls to the BZÖ.]] So: The question of the future security of the social state in Austria is an essential question, is one that the two major parties in this new Austrian Federal Government take very seriously, which has led to a number of social policy projects in the Government Convention, which you are discussing today and tomorrow here in the House. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Somehow you're not there!]] The most important of these is a committed full employment policy. This also includes the fact that for more employment, income security and income levels – including minimum wages – are more important than they have been so far – secured by a need-oriented minimum guarantee in order to limit down transfers. – That’s our answer! This is a modern, progressive and humane response to the need for the social state. ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e10282	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! We have a package of amendments on federal constitution and eight other laws, all of which concern the right to vote, the right to vote, which is without a doubt the heart of democracy and which, in Austria, has been free, secret, personal, for men for over 100 years, is somewhat shorter for women. And we are happy to discuss and decide today also to extend the circle of electors in the form of the electoral age reduction to 16 years. This is, in any case, a very, very pleasant day from the point of view of the Greens. It took many years to realize that young people 16 years of age are not only immature to choose, but also have a right to participate in the instruments that shape their world of life. However, this issue has become a political exchange business today. For years, the ÖVP has resisted the reduction of the electoral age to 16 resistance and has now replaced it against the election of letters. At the beginning of the 1990s, we Greens were here with requests and tried to explain with arguments why young people should not be excluded from the direct co-determination of the National Council, the Land Days and the direct democratic instruments. From our point of view, it is a very, very encouraging signal that there is a reduction in the electoral age today. We also see it as the result of persistent work over many years, in which we Greens have repeatedly submitted applications for electoral age reduction, but which have been rejected. This means that 16-year-olds can also exercise their right in the elections of Europe, in the elections of the National Council, in the elections of the Landtags, in the elections of the Federal President and in all direct-democratic instruments. And there is no need to be – I quote now a very bizarre comparison that the alleged hope for the future of the ÖVP – capable of winning the four-span tour, but it is a natural extension of the circle of the electorate who has long, long, long been overdue. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] It is also gratifying that 18-year-olds can now be candidates for the National Council and for the European Parliament. This was an important innovation in the course of the committee negotiations, which we also welcome very clearly, clearly and loudly. We also welcome the choice of letters. Here, too, there have been some skeptical voices in the past, but the choice of letters is a very important instrument to take account of the increasing mobility of the Austrian population. I do not think that there is a fundamental presumption to this affidavit which is intended to secure the secret and personal right to vote. By the way, it is probably just a quantitative change, because the right of foreigners to vote was already a form of the right of choice of letter. So you just have a larger number of people who can perceive it. Of course, this will also lead more people to participate. I'm convinced of that. The reliefs for foreign-Austrian and foreign-Austrian women are also positive. We have also tabled proposals which are now largely being dealt with. That's what we're looking forward to. The falling away of the witnesses, the possibility of ordering a electoral ticket subscription for ten years – all of this is very, very positive and serves to remove technical obstacles to the people who wish to see themselves as citizens in foreign countries through the right to vote with Austria. However, I am afraid that the form that the Committee has chosen, in which the municipalities are only asked to use high-speed postal services, is not a guarantee that the electoral cards will always arrive in time. We would have liked it if it had been introduced as mandatory to use rapid mail services so that no voice could actually be lost on the post. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] However, the electoral reform remains half-way. It is a bit unfortunate and worth mentioning that many people in Austria are still excluded from the right to vote – although directly affected by it. With this I address an obstacle that has been discussed in Vienna for a long time. In Vienna, the SPÖ and the Greens have jointly contributed to the extension of voting rights to people who do not have Austrian citizenship – at least at local level. And that's where I want to go. They are really seriously asking if this is such an improper approach that people who live for years in a city, a community cannot even say the zebra strips at their doorstep, although they are directly affected by these daily decisions made by a councillor. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] [[Abg. Dr. Graf: It's about citizenship!]] This discussion is quite common in other countries, I ask you to just think about what democracy means. Democracy means that those affected by a decision can also influence them. And especially with regard to the local problems, the solutions that a community provides to its citizens, it is about everyday problems, everyday things, and here a large group of people is excluded. These are, by the way, very young people in Austria. Many – more than half – of these so-called foreigners are under 25. If we were to talk about youth partitioning and better integration, the municipal right to vote for people with non-Austrian citizenship would also have been an important point. In Carinthia, too, the right to vote is such that it excludes a minority, namely that of the Slovenians from a representation in the country day. With this reform, the opportunity would have existed to force a right of vote in Carinthia – after decades of not being able to create such a one – which would open up the opportunity for a very large group of the population to speak. Unfortunately, this reform has not been achieved either. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] This is soluble with discipline. You can limit electoral costs and have government negotiations serious in four to six weeks. It is not necessary to limit the rights of citizens. Regrettably, there was no discussion about this at all. The parties who now represent this have not spoken in the election campaign of trying to cut the right to the citizens, nor have they sought a serious discussion on the harmonisation of the entire legislative periods – in the country and so on. If you really take the argument seriously to want to work undisturbed, then you also have to say that the country elections – whether in Lower Austria or Upper Austria – are very often blocking and delaying political projects at the federal level, because there is something to be expected of a head of state at the moment. - It all went wrong. It is therefore only right and cheap that, if I take part in this cycle, I can also choose those representatives who then determine what they want to do with my tax money. I think that this reduction in the electoral age is extremely reasonable. It also means an extension of the constituency of around 180 000 people in this overall package, so that it cannot be said that democracy has been restricted because, on the other hand, it leads to an extension of the population that is allowed to vote. I think it is balanced and extremely reasonable that a larger population should be brought into the elections. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Do you want minimum pensioners not to choose? - They don't pay taxes!]] Let's go to the introduction of the letter election: We were always very skeptical about this issue. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Do you want only those who pay taxes to vote?]] – That is true, Mr Commissioner, but I believe that we have succeeded in building up a number of points in the text of the Constitution, which also make it difficult to abuse – although we can never completely prevent it, but at least make it difficult. The criteria must be set out, which is why one makes use of the choice of letters – namely, local absence, health reasons or foreign stay – and there is a need for an application where this reason is to be stated, and there is a need for proof of identity. This is in the text of the Constitution. It also states that there must be an affidavit that the vote is personal and secret. An affidavit is also always associated with criminal measures in the event of abuse, so that some security features have been specifically incorporated here in the selection of letters, which are ultimately intended to prevent abuse. In the National Council election, these criteria were reinforced once again by requiring a further affidavit that this post-election took place at a certain time, namely before the constituency was closed. Both statements serve to prevent abuse, and I believe that the most advanced restriction criteria have been found here in order to vote in favour of a choice of letter. You now have the opportunity to get rid of a choice card: With this dial you can choose in your own local, select in the dial card location or exercise the letter dial function. There has been an increase in the need for identity in the National Council election, so I believe that many criteria have been created here to prevent abuse. We will never be able to completely exclude abuse, but I believe that protection has been as far as possible. Some explanations on the other possibilities: We also support the facilitation of the choice of foreign Austrians; that we finally also allow OSCE election observers is, I believe, also reasonable, and that we have also blessed a number of motions for resolutions which are intended to bring about a harmonisation by clarifying the rules in the electoral order. [[Abg. Strache: That would be reasonable!]] [[Abg. Dr. Graf: If this continues, we will need it!]] In this context, there is an amendment tabled by Mrs Wittmann, Mr Spindelegger, Mr Glavischnig and Mr Scheibner. I can explain its core points: Without changing the legal provisions concerning the composition of the Federal Electoral Authority, after the distribution according to the d’Hondt’s system, the BZÖ would have fallen out of the Federal Electoral Authority – we find that unfair. A compromise has been reached in the Committee's deliberations, so that the BZÖ is now also represented in the Federal Electoral Authority. This amendment was tabled by us. In general, the electoral reform is balanced with three very large milestones: Reduction of the electoral age, the introduction of the letter election and, ultimately, the extension of the legislative period, and I say that this extension of the legislative period is accompanied by a very comprehensive discussion of control rights. In this context, we are still of the opinion that one of the most comprehensive control rights would also be to grant minorities the request for the setting up of an investigation committee. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: But he's not there!]] [[Abg. Dr. Moser: Why don't you do it?]] – In general, the electoral reform is very balanced and beneficial.

In the past, and especially in recent weeks, I have had a lot of discussions with young people who are very willing to take an interest in politics, are willing to make use of the right to vote. It is also true that we need to consider how far we can make political education even more possible in schools. The second point, a crucial point, is the introduction of the choice of letters. The mailbox becomes a constituency, regardless of the constituency and the opening hours. This makes choosing even easier, even more flexible and faster. The elections can now be carried out in Germany and abroad by post, wherever you want, in addition, of course in your own community as before or in every municipality in Austria. In addition, there is still the possibility with the flying Commission. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: This is a false claim!]] We must note that, of course, people are becoming more flexible and mobile and that the planning of weekend appointments and holiday planning are not always aligned to the election day. That is why it is necessary, right and important that we offer this possibility of choice of letter in the future. Since we have held the debate today on the fact that voters are actually deliberately held to abuse or that they commit a criminal act, I would like to say one thing: I trust the electorate, and I certainly do not accuse them of abuse! [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! I say this for the following reason: For 17 years, we have had the possibility that the Austrians from abroad can choose with the electoral card. In all these 17 years, there was no case of abuse. If we trust the electorate, we will ultimately not abuse them! I am glad that we are also making special reliefs for the Austrians of abroad more Austrians. It is important that we no longer have this difficult procedure, this complicated procedure with witnesses and the like. It is necessary that we also make an offer to the Austrians abroad. It is true that an official understanding is now carried out if one has been included in the electorate. The Auslandsösterreichers also have the opportunity to order a subscription that they will automatically receive the electoral card in the next ten years, so that they can make use of the right to vote. This is an important point that we provide a service here for the Austrians abroad. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] Dear ladies and gentlemen! Strache: Expand Direct Democracy!]] I consider it extremely important that we also make it possible to vote on the Internet in the future, i.e. e-voting, and I justify this as follows: [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] Austria is the world leader in the eGovernment. To sum up, we have a good electoral reform package for more democracy in Austria, and I ask everyone to agree to this bill.

It is precisely the choice of letters that brings a clear increase in democracy, because no group of voters will be excluded from the vote from the vote from the outset because of their absence on the election day. This is particularly important for Vorarlberg, because Vorarlberg is a country that does not have a university. Until now, our students had to take long journeys and therefore also costs to get to the constituencies to do their vote. On the basis of this new choice of letters, they will be saved from this unnecessary effort and the costs. It is a pity that this possibility of the choice of letters has been blocked for so long. Simplifications in voting for Austrians from abroad are also very encouraging. The complicated procedure of voting in an Austrian representative authority abroad in front of a witness is now missing. The reduction in the electoral age throughout the country to 16 means more democracy and for us politicians, as has been mentioned more often, more attention to young people. It is urgent to intensify political education in schools in order to prepare our young people for the possibility of political participation. I see a great advantage in extending the legislative period to five years. This period of time has proved successful in our federal states as well as in our communities. In summary, I note: This electoral reform brings more democracy, it brings more political participation of young people and encouraging simplification. It is therefore to be welcomed.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e15438	Madam President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I am very glad that this anti-doping matter has ended up in a five-party application and finally a five-party arrangement can be adopted here. I think this shows outwardly that we, as legislators, are self-interested in doping combat. This is also a signal towards the Olympic application of Salzburg, but it is also a signal to Europe that we are adopting one of the most modern doping laws that are currently in place in Europe. It is a shame that false reports are also available in the media regarding this Olympic application, because the claim that Salzburg had become the latter is not true, but Salzburg has the same name as an excellent application as the South Korean application, only Sochi has not received the same assessment. And that's why news releases and media news are simply wrong today. I don't understand the reason. It is a pity because it is certainly not helpful for this application to make an incorrect media coverage. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Dr. stupid.]] On the subject itself. . I believe that the principle to which we have agreed not to criminalise the athlete, but to punish the environment is a principle that is increasingly applied in Europe. Even countries such as France, Spain, Italy and Belgium, which have been subject to criminal law, go away from it again. In Italy, the debate is already beginning, that we want to get out of criminal law into a system similar to ours. In Germany, criminal law has been adapted in such a way that it has been created precisely those criminal offences that we have already regulated in the Medicines Act, and those criminal offences that we have removed and placed under the anti-doping law have not been covered by criminal law. That means that we are actually in the international trend. We have here the principle of the persecution of the environment and the non-punitive persecution of the active sportsman. What does that mean? – This means that the athlete does not have a free letter on doping, but his punishment or sanction is that he cannot participate in competitions because he is locked, and thus loses his livelihood in the top sports. I think that is punishment enough, and it is not necessary to punish the application itself here. It is a right principle that the environment is hit with full hardness. If you know today's doping methods, you also know that they are no longer possible without scientific supervision, without medical supervision, and then you also know that the most modern doping methods are always accompanied. And this is to be put under the most severe punishment. You can work here with the criminal instruments. If there is a suspicion that a doping agent has been administered, applied or passed on, you will of course have the entire criminal instruments available because it is subject to punishment in the Medicines Act, and you can also have home searches and other procedures carried out. It is therefore not true to argue that only if you put the athlete under criminal law would you have these means. This can also be achieved by punishing the environment. That is to say, you have all the forced measures available, but you have found a way that ultimately does not criminalize all athletes who have once resorted to a doping agent. I also believe that it is a difference how to take a doping agent, whether this happens by mistake at times, because one takes in contaminated batches of food supplements, or whether one makes it aware of certain scientific methods. I believe that this is a gratification, but in this context I believe it has been a proper and directional decision. We have two independent bodies, which are now also issuing sanctions. We have also included in the anti-doping law that these instances must take care of prevention. Funding has also been made available for this purpose. I believe that this is the right way, and international development is right here. It must be said that in these associations, for example in the Schiverband, which has been miscredited, 362 samples took place last year and were all negative. That means, essentially, our athletes are clean. And that there are always some black sheep, you know, and these were also punished accordingly. That's right. We are now putting an instrument into the hands of the associations. We give the state, independent bodies an instrument that allows efficient persecution of doping sinners and also efficient punishment of the environment and the sportsman through sports sanctions. I think we are creating a modern, efficient means of combating doping.

We now have clear provisions in an independent anti-doping agency, where I expect a management that is to be designated to be carried out in both the first and the second instances, when it comes to proceedings, to be carried out in the best possible way. That was a problem so far. The various associations had very different rules in the procedural provisions: the one according to the criminal procedure, the other according to civil procedural regulations, and the third had very peculiar arbitration procedures. This means: a uniform standard for all athletes with two instances and then, of course, the way to the sports court in Lausanne, to the CAS. This is a very important difference from the previous regulations. The next point that I expect a lot of quality improvement is that we will set up three commissions: a medical commission, a legal commission and – which is very important – because sports has gone away in many areas, because the desire to win at all costs has overlaid everything else – also an ethics commission. With this advice, I expect the management to do more in the anti-doping field and that this movement in Austria will become much more noticeable than it has been in the past. The crucial point is this – you see it in the explanations: So far, we had available between €500 000 and € 600 000 for this entire area. We double this amount: In the future, there will be far more than € 1 million for this. This is very crucial if you really want to do this efficiently. We don't leave loopholes open either, although you always have to see both poles here. One pole is to do everything to stop doping, not to leave loopholes here, on the other hand, athletes of course also have a family environment, or they are under enormous tension when they prepare for Olympic games. If regular nightly checks are carried out, this is certainly not the optimal training preparation. That is why we will certainly continue to deal with this issue in the future. The revision shall be in accordance with the Basel Convention on the Control of Transboundary Movements. The main changes in short form: In the future, there will be two waste lists instead of three, i.e. the green ones for non-notificational waste and waste mixes and the yellow list of waste subject to notification. Notifications must now be submitted uniformly to the competent authority at the place of dispatch in all Member States. There are now also clear rules for the security performance to be submitted in the event that a shipment, recovery or disposal cannot be properly completed. Persistent organic pollutants, so-called POP waste, can in future be stored on special landfills if this makes sense. In the case of improved data protection schemes, electronic data management should be used more as a tool for notifications, for applications, but also for ads. Another important step towards eGovernment, and I'm sure it's really an advantage if you leave this paper economy behind. Producers of hazardous waste will also be included in the registration obligation in the future. At the cost: The innovations are leading to costs of €7,628 in the federal budget, a total of €880 000 per year. However, from 2010 onwards, it is expected that savings in the order of € 1 million can be achieved annually due to simplifications in the eGovernment and in particular by electronic investigations. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! If in the past one had become more or more sensitive to these registrations or if one had made these registrations better, then we would not have to bear these high costs of the old load repairs that we have to carry today. That is why I welcome the new Waste Management Act.

If I follow the debate this way, I can only be surprised: for the Greens, far too little, for other opposition parties – specifically the BZÖ – much too much, there will be a little of the €500 million left. I'll tell you: The €500 million by 2010 are unique, compared to other benchmarks in Europe! Nearly 7 billion schilling by 2010 fresh, new money for climate and energy policy in Austria – this can be seen! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the SPÖ.]] We acted very quickly. In March, with the climate strategy for Austria, we set out the objectives where the individual local authorities, where we all together as consumers have a mammoth task ahead of us in order to achieve the climate change targets by 2012. We will only achieve them together, not in one another, but in each other's sphere. The Climate and Energy Fund – that is unique – will be able to use the funds to provide three major programme lines: for the first time, research and development in the field of climate and energy policy. I have just returned from the informal Council of Environment Ministers in Essen, Germany, under the German Presidency – two days of very intense discussion, which was very much in line with the question: What technology will enable the environmental policy of the future to foster quality of life and environmental benefits worldwide? Jeremy Rifkin was there and said: We are facing an ecological-industrial revolution, the door to a new energy age is being launched. If we take this very, very seriously – and I do – then we want to do business not only with environmental benefits, but with this trend, with this question of energy, a new energy age, with ecological industrialization, the third great wave, so to speak. And this is an exciting point that an environmental politician needs to take seriously – to make environmental policy with home mind – is a guiding principle from me – namely to find economic and environmental answers. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The Climate and Energy Fund provides funds for these three programme lines, i.e. first for research and development at the universities of smart projects, for projects coming from the private sector. Secondly, We also want to highlight public transport. I do not understand the criticism in this area at all. When we see that mobility in this country, that the behaviour of people gives us the biggest problem – twice as much CO2 emissions since 1990 through the use of cars and trucks – then we must also support and force an increase in the attractiveness of public transport, because it has incredible climate relevance, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] So the second big pillar is: In the area of local transport, attractiveness increases. There is already a very interesting gas pipeline from Norway, which contributes significantly, and there is also a project that has already been discussed here, in which the OMV from Iran wants to bring natural gas to Europe. There are all these possibilities, but all these are transition phases into a new energy age, as the Federal Minister has rightly said. However, in one season he was able to repair only two sides of his hotel, saying: equip with thermal insulation. This entrepreneur then charged this investment to the municipal credit, but received the notice from the local credit: No, you don't get the promised promotion because you didn't reach the energy code of 70 kilowatt hours per square meter! The disappointment was, of course, great because the entrepreneur rightly stated: I am not able to equip and cover the entire hotel in an intermediate season! He has now put this investment in part, but has fallen over the funding. Since I am asking not only to raise expectations in the private sector, but to do much more sensitive in the future, because the potential is so great especially in thermal building renovation. An older house, which has not yet been renovated, offers a 50 percent energy savings potential. And if you know how many houses, hotels, businesses are not yet renovated throughout Austria, you can say: This is truly a huge potential! Mr Minister, I would ask you to help this businessman who has taken a step in the right direction. He was able to tell the media over and over again that so much money is being made available for energy saving and for future energy potentials. But if he now looks into his balance sheet, he must find that he lacks the promised promotion he will not get now. So I would like to ask you to proceed more sensitively in the future in order to be able to benefit much better from the enormous potential of thermal building renovation. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e20875	The next speaker is Mr Hornek. 2 minutes of voluntary time limit. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e20896	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! If we want to get under control of global global warming, we need to fundamentally change our energy systems: to reduce consumption, to increase efficiency and to reduce fossil energy sources. Parliament today has the draft law on the establishment of a climate and energy fund – a law that will make a significant contribution to the achievement of sustainable energy supply by increasing energy efficiency and by increasing the share of renewable energy sources. § 1 of this Act defines the objective: to increase the share of renewable energy sources in total consumption from 25 percent to 2010 and from 45 percent to 2020. With This fund continues Austria's path towards achieving sustainable energy supply. The establishment of the Climate and Energy Fund will provide strong impetus for the Austrian environmental technology sector and an important contribution to the environmental and climate strategy. Rural areas are the main sufferers of climate change. However, rural areas are at the same time an important key to solving the problem. From here the energy of the future can come. Biomass production is an essential pillar of sustainable climate-friendly supply of renewable energy. The Climate and Energy Fund will be spent up to €500 million for the period 2007 to 2010. For 2007, €50 million will be made available as part of a federal financial authorisation and €150 million for 2008. For the years 2009 and 2010, up to €150 million are also estimated annually. The benefits of the Fund can be clearly defined: First: Further expansion of our technology leadership in the field of environmental technology through research. Secondly, Increased security of supply in the energy sector for Austria. Thirdly, As mentioned today, up to 12,000 new jobs are created, according to a Wifo study. A wise law, a right step in the right direction! – Accept!

ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e21213	We're now on the 9th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. We are therefore immediately entering the debate. Mrs Schönpass is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Voluntary time limit: 2 minutes. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e21244	Mr President. Mr Pröll! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! The present marketing standard law has been overdue for a long time. Although the nature, size, packaging and labelling of certain agricultural products have also been regulated in the Austrian Quality Class Act, The law dates back to 1967, and in particular in recent years it has had to be very often innovationized, especially in order to take account of European regulations. A single and comprehensive legal basis is established, in particular, to implement all Community marketing standards, with the result of the current marketing standards law. It is new in substance that standards for the marketing of olive oil, fishery and aquaculture products and milk products are also regulated directly and not through regulations. These marketing standards are, in principle, of immense importance. They promote domestic competitive quality products, facilitate the movement of goods between different levels of trade and facilitate competition between domestic and foreign goods. And fortunately, ladies and gentlemen, we do not need to shy this competition. I therefore appeal to you all: Voices You agree to this bill! Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e21280	The next speaker is Mr Deputie's friend. 3 minutes of voluntary time limit. Please, my colleague.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e21301	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! With the present Marketing Standards Act, we today decide to adapt to existing EU law. The Austrian Quality Class Law of 1967 was no longer in time despite numerous novels and is thus replaced by the present law. Quality standards, trading classes, selling or marketing standards for agricultural products are now combined under the term ‘marketing standards’ and are governed by a new law; Mrs Schönpass has already pointed out this in her statement. The aim of the new scheme is to establish a uniform and comprehensive legal basis for Community marketing standards. And this is important for the agricultural industry, for trade and for consumers. However, it is also a matter of laying down principles for the introduction of food from third countries not covered by Community legislation. This includes the packaging and labelling of the goods as well as the monitoring of compliance with these provisions. Ladies and gentlemen, the Marketing Standards Act takes account of a proper classification of agricultural products. In the context of this amendment, I would also like to point out that there is a great deal of transparency in food production among our farmers. The procedures at the farm are the most precise, and strict conditions in terms of animal and environmental protection must be observed, which is also controlled. Those who buy food produced in Austria can be sure that they take high-quality and healthy products. I think that the final consumer also has a right to transparency on the food shelf. To be welcomed would be a uniform labelling of all final products. The consumer today wants to know where the original product comes from as such. My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen, this is also very much in the interest of the Austrian agricultural sector. A small country like Austria can be in sharp constituency. There is no curfew against the large agricultural countries by means of price struggle. We must therefore place massive emphasis on product and environmental quality. Strict controls on the import of organic products from third countries should also be sought, because in food sold in Austria, what is in it is really what is on it. The Austrian Farmers' Federation, also the Agricultural Market Austria and the Ministry of Life have been working intensively for many years to ensure a positive marketing of food from Austria. In this context, for example, I should like to recall the labelling initiative for milk and milk products in the context of the “The Taste of Homeland” campaign. I think we are on the right track to be internationally competitive, and in this direction we must continue to work. Thank you.

People need something urgently, they usually have to organize and finance themselves in their need, most often they lend themselves the money because they don't have it, and if they then seek funding for this aid, then they won't get the money afterwards. Then it always says that they should have applied before they have organized and bought the wheelchair or shower chair or whatever. And this, ladies and gentlemen, is impossible, because I can't wait for three months for a shower chair and don't shower in the meantime. But if I want to take a shower before, I have to pay for the piece myself – then I already bought it before I submit it. So these are practices that are not tolerable, but we experience them again and again. And, ladies and gentlemen, this practice is also illegal, as the People's Procuratorate points out very clearly, but it is still being done again and again. I have also taken this opportunity this time again and asked your corresponding question; I am curious what will come this time as an answer. If one needs disability-related grants – in any form whatsoever – then one actually assumes that the applications should be done quickly and unbureaucraticly, that there is a quick help. The opposite is the case: If you are looking for such benefits, it takes a very long time and it is very, very laborious to come to aid or money. Very often people don't need this anymore, because there are cases where people die again and again before they get their necessary aid at all. An area that raised the waves last week in the Committee is the area of socio-economic enterprises or the situation of people with disabilities working in employment therapy facilities. The People's Procuratorate also points out, ladies and gentlemen of the government parties, that it is absolutely a trap trick – that is called by the People's Procuratorate – that people with disabilities who work in these institutions achieve an income below the social security limit. This means that they can't claim unemployment benefit, acquire pension et cetera and only be held over pocket money. When I presented it to the committee last week, it was pretended that this was not true, so to speak: Where did she come from?! – This is to be read on page 273 in the People’s Prosecution Report; for all those who still don’t believe it. One area where there are always problems and which I always mention is family support for present and civil servants. These people simply don't get them, although it would be possible from the law. I could now list you a lot of situations of life that have been mentioned in the public prosecutor's reports in 2005 and 2006, and I promise you, ladies and gentlemen, I will not tire of showing these points again and again, and not let them go, because it cannot be that the public prosecutorship – for which we do not have them – is, although it makes recommendations and tries to change something in the minds of people, then every year before the same fact is put: You can write what you want, but we'll leave everything as it is! I will make sure that some points are corrected again. I know it's a hard fight, but I'm going to lead this fight again. I would like to thank you, Mrs Bauer, for wishing you all the best, let it go well, do all that you didn't have time for now! I think we're going to see each other more often, but we live in the same district – at the latest at the bus stop we meet regularly. Mrs Bauer is also a public driver, just by the way. [[Assembly with the Greens and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] I would also like to thank Mr Stadler very much for his work. I was very suspicious of you in the beginning, but I have to say it worked halfway, at least for me. [[Abg. May. Mr Kabas, whom I hardly really know, I also wish you every good and good time, whatever you are planning to do in your retirement. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e22916	The next item is the report by Mr Steier, a two-minute speaker. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e22937	Madam President. Estimated Volksanwälte! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! [[Abg. Pepper: And Volksanwalt in, Gerhard!]] Today, we debate the reports of the People's Procuratorate 2005 and 2006, which describe individual cases on several hundred pages, which are the basis of the Volksanwälte. On this occasion, as has been said many times before, I would like to say a warm thank you to the ladies and gentlemen, but also to the many employees for their commitment to the service of citizens. Ladies and gentlemen, a permanent burner at the People's Procuratorate, in both the 2005 and 2006 reports, is the 3-2 count rule in school buses, which causes three children under the age of 14 to count as two people. For years parents have been demanding more safety for children in school buses, and at least for the same time experts point to the unsatisfactory and dangerous legal situation in the field of the transport of children in school buses or other means of transport to the kindergarten. Schoolchildren push, packed with heavy school bags, in overcrowded buses and then have to share a two-seat seat with thirds of school bags, without safety belts. I can quote from the report: “This regulation poses an increased risk of injury, for example in the case of emergency brakes, as children under 14 years of age can be transported daily to and from schools and kindergartens by means of children under 14 years of age and under 50 seats and 45 seats.” – Quotes. Ladies and gentlemen, there has finally been a created order! Austria is one of the few countries in the European Union that still has this 3-2 count rule. I now also see my citizens' initiative, which has had 7 000 signatures in the Burgenland, supported by this prominent report in the People's Prosecution Report. I urge the current government not to go the way of the last government, but to put this really necessary act of liberation from the 3:2 counting rule and to implement the 1:1 counting rule so that at last all parents and children are satisfied and the students can be safely brought to school with a safe school bus. - Thank you.

Attendances and civilians shall not receive family allowances for the period of their presence or civil service. In practice, this state support for presence or civil service has often not been sufficient to actually cover the cost of living. If an additional effort is needed for a study, the parents still have to get in the bag properly. Students may earn €8,725 a year; a present or civil servant with much less income loses the right to family support. In addition, for the families concerned, other benefits, such as multiple child allowances in the family allowance or in the multi-child allowance, are also lost with the absence of the aid. Ladies and gentlemen, this shows that low-income families are severely disadvantaged. Our next goal must therefore be to change that.

Point Report by the Financial Committee on Amendment No 220/A by Mr Gabriele Tamandl, Mr Kai Jan Krainer, Members of the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy, on the proposal from the Commission to the Council (Doc. of the Act amending the Income Tax Act 1988, the Income Tax Act 1994 and the General Social Insurance Act (Travel Cost Novelle 2007 – RK-Novelle 2007) (123 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e25390	We're coming to the 14th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. We are going into the debate. Mr Haberzettl was the first to speak. 2 minutes welcome. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e25421	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Mr State Secretary! Dear ladies and gentlemen! This novelle was necessary as a result of a finding of the Constitutional Court. In this, the § 26 Income Tax Act and the Travel Cost Regulation based on it have been repealed. The repealed provisions provided for under certain conditions that the daily allowance could also be paid as a tax-free reimbursement to workers if the place of employment was already the focus of the activity. The same was true in the area of travel costs. The present amendment ensures that, following the recognition of the Constitutional Court, there is no financial deterioration of this group of workers. However, certain rules need to be re-regulated. These include, in particular, provisions in the Income Tax Act, in the Sales Tax Act and also individual provisions in the ASVG. The Income Tax Act now lists in § 3 those offences which can continue to be tax-free by virtue of income-forming rules. In addition, Article 26 of the Income Tax Act provides for a new regime which can also be tax-free from professional costs, which are not affected by the shuttle fee or by the amount of the transport deduction, but which are subject to a certain distance. The present solution is to be welcomed, on the one hand, in the sense of the workers, on the other, but also in the sense of the economy. There is an agreement between the social partners on the whole subject. I would therefore ask you to support this draft.

In concrete terms, he has the fourth sentence of § 26 para. 4 Income Tax Act, which is the following – and it is important to point it out; I quote –: Contains a wage-forming provision within the meaning of § 68 para. 5 Z 1 to 6 Income tax law a special regulation of the concept of mission is to be applied. - Quote. The abolition of this seemingly harmless rate has the result that the historically grown remuneration system will be eliminated in a number of sectors, especially in the construction industry, with the result that a number of workers, in particular construction workers, would have to lose net wages, because this would in particular require daily diets of €26.04. Of course, from the Greens' point of view, these net wage losses are unbearable for those affected. That's not a question! Now, for a year, a new constitutional regime has been considered. What happened? – An initiative proposal with a probably new unconstitutional solution has come out! I'll try to justify that. The new regulation is essentially equal to the existing legislation. New is just the location of the scheme. Thus, tax payments paid as travel expenses are tax-free – and I now quote from the own-initiative application – as far as the employer is on the basis of a wage-forming requirement in accordance with § 68 para. 5 ZZ 1 to 6 is obliged to pay. – So widespread coverage equality with what the VfGH has lifted! I would ask you, Mr State Secretary, to explain to me why this regulation should now be constitutional! Professor However, Doralt considers the application to be unconstitutional for a second reason. I quote: If the explanatory statement also explicitly points out that the law is intended to safeguard the economic location of the sectors affected by it, that is to say, the safeguarding of the location of the favoured sectors is at the expense of the unfavourable locations or industries. ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e25938	This is what Mrs Maij-Weggen has to say. 2 minutes welcome. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e25960	Madam President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The revision of the travel fee is based on a knowledge of the Constitutional Court. The Travel Cost Novelle 2007 is a repair of the § 26 Income Tax Act. There are also minor changes to the VAT Act and ASVG. The reason for the cancellation was that daily payments were also paid as non-taxable costs to workers when the place of employment was already a centre of activity in accordance with the general rules of the place of employment. The same was true of the cost of travel. The tax-free payment of daily payments has always been a wage component. A loss would result in significant net wage losses for employees in certain sectors. For employees whose work is, for example, to spend hours in the field of foreign service, or to perform construction and assembly activities or to be left as workers, the present amendment ensures that these funds can continue to be paid tax-free. In addition, it is also laid down that professional-induced travel costs, which are not covered by the shuttle fee or the amount of the transport deduction, will be left free of tax. This revision of the travel fee establishes a constitutional system for daily allowances and travel expenses in the sense of equal treatment for all taxable persons.

The present Federal Law on the performance of an Austrian contribution to the fourth replenishment of the Global Environmental Facility Trust Fund provides for more than €24 million to be made available for the next four years. The idea that has existed since 1991 is to support developing and transformation countries in addressing global environmental problems. This idea is also good and fair, because these are those countries that are more affected by the damage and disasters than they themselves are responsible for their causes. That is why it is also important to pay attention to the reasonable implementation of these GEF projects. We are particularly concerned with respect to the Austrian anti-atomic course, because it is not biofuel cultivation, for example, that does not lead to monocultures that genetic engineering is not used, that rain forest deforestation does not occur, that it does not come through monocultures, for example, to an increase in food prices. It is equally important to ensure that the social impact, human rights, the habitat of indigenous peoples et cetera is observed when implementing them. The second area to which I would like to say something today is the double taxation agreement in the case of profit adjustments between related companies, with which we today understand, in the case of ten countries, namely the ten new Member States of the European Union, the change, almost accession to the European Union. I do not think that it is specifically against the plan to make this conciliation procedure; it is good and right. However, what we should really consider is whether this type of ratification process, as we experience it here – that is, to do it again and again for a large number of countries – really makes sense and whether it would not be more sensible, better and more efficient to delegate it to the level of the European Union instead of leaving it at the level of the Member States.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e26367	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Today, we liberals will not give our consent to the re-filling of the Global Environment Facility Trust Fund. My colleague Dr. Kurzmann has already explained this shortly. We are making this clear and not because we want to question or disavow this objective. This is about aid against climate change, which is to be found at 33 percent, for biodiversity, for international waters and so on. Our attitude against the padding of this fund is not to disavow these contents, but to what extent these funds, which Austria has paid for over a decade, are efficient. We would also like to refer to the report of the Financial Committee on the Government Template, which reads as follows: The aim is to improve the strategic Guidance and direction of success by reviewing and adapting these areas which I have just mentioned. This should also be the objective of the Austrian Federal Government, Secretary of State, that it review in all these areas of development cooperation, in which form an efficiency can be determined after all these years in which Austria pumps in money. It is also slightly more than €24 million for this refill. We are free to pay attention to two areas and represent their interests: firstly, to the area of Austrian taxpayers who are asked to pay the cash to fill these funds; and secondly, to the area of the people concerned in these countries. If one considers very sober the successes of developing countries in the last 50 years, one has to say that there is very little efficiency there. We Liberals do not agree to this in order to encourage the Federal Government to check efficiency here, not only in this one area, but in principle.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-06-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00024_d8e26431	Madam President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! I would like to make a few comments on Agenda item 17, the accession of the new Member States to an existing arbitration agreement to resolve settlement price problems between related companies within the EU – within the EU! It really seems to be a problem, therefore, that internationally active related companies can move profits from one state of the European Union to another state of the European Union by autonomous setting of transfer prices. There is therefore a separate arbitration agreement which provides for a special procedure for resolving settlement price problems, which the new Member States of the European Union have now joined. I think that is a good thing, and we will therefore agree to this government proposal. Nevertheless, something remains open to me, which makes me think: So there is a problem with settlement prices. This has been recognized and resolved within the EU – yes, once again: within the EU! But what about international companies that are also active outside the European Union? If, therefore, such an agreement is necessary within the EU, then the mandatory reversal can only be: Internationally active companies, which are also active outside the EU, therefore, if there is no agreement, are delaying their losses in terms of their desire and mood between countries by means of transfer prices. Then these losses can be offset by domestic profits, and in the end no cent tax is paid in Austria, thanks to a Europe-wide rather unique group taxation, which was introduced under black and blue. Therefore, I ask myself the question: When will this group tax finally be assessed? How high is the tax loss for the Republic? And why should citizens now be economical in such a generous advantage for businesses if the companies can juggle like wild and thus escape any domestic tax duty? I'm not expecting any advance from the ÖVP. After all, the ÖVP has never made any sense of its conviction that the favour of large corporations, of multi-earners, of friends in the Industrial Union, of large heirs is at the heart of their policy, and it also has no interest in an ecological-social tax reform. But from the SPÖ, yes from the SPÖ, I am disillusioned. Who was it in the election campaign up land, dropped the message down that the group tax will be abolished as soon as you're on the row of power? Well, we remind ourselves that it was you, Mr State Secretary, but also the current Federal Chancellor. Who else? And what happens now? - Nothing happens! Nothing happens! [[Interlocutory note by Secretary of State Dr. Matznetter.]] Mr State Secretary! There is not even the approach of an initiative to seriously evaluate group taxation, to not even evaluate it, let alone abolish it. And that, Mr State Secretary, I do not think honestly. – Thank you very much.
